S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I get so much energy that I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and rewrite care schedules all in the same night without being able to sit down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I’ve gone many nights sleeping maybe two or three hours, then getting up feeling charged like I had a full rest and going straight back to painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. And I try to ignore it, you know, like maybe it’s the neighbors or the TV, but I don’t even have anything on sometimes. The other home-care agency, and some people from the library too, like they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to steal some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nAnd I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but… see, the library people know everybody, they see what you check out, and I used to go there to look at art books sometimes. Anyway, I just knew they were doing it. I could feel it, like they were putting things together behind my back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts were being placed in your mind that aren't your own, or that events or actions around you were specifically directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head, like… I can’t say it that way exactly. Yes… yes, that’s kind of what I mean. Like I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some of those people from the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, even my client papers… like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.

I don’t know if it was like the TV or radio talking straight to me… I don’t really watch the same shows every day, and sometimes the commercials all sound the same anyway. But I did feel like things around me were aimed at me, yes.

I don’t think controlled, no… not like somebody had a remote on me or anything. And thoughts being put in my head, I don’t know. I get thoughts, same as anybody, and sometimes I think too much, especially when I’m tired or when people keep asking me things and I’m trying to explain it right.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.

With my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.

Because I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.

But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I got it in my head that the other home-care agency and some people from the library were watching my paintings and client papers to steal a chance that was meant for me, and even when folks told me it made no sense I still knew they were doing it.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.

And see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I used to get art books there too, and then I’d think, well, now they know what I’m working on.

So yes, I thought they were talking about me and trying to take an opportunity that was meant for me. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.

I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.

People kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.

mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs about yourself that others might not understand, such as thinking you have special powers, a special mission, or are someone important that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I used to get art books there too, and then I’d think, well, now they know what I’m working on.\n\nSo yes, I thought they were talking about me and trying to take an opportunity that was meant for me. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.\n\nAnd I know people said, “That doesn’t make sense, why would they do that,” but at the time I was sure. I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there times when you feel completely certain about something happening to you or being planned against you, even when others say there's no evidence it's true?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… that’s what I mean. I got really certain that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching what I was doing.

It started with my paintings, but then it felt like they were noticing my client papers too, like they were putting things together and trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. And I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d try to hear that, but I still felt like… no, I know what I’m seeing.

The library thing bothered me because I used to go there for art books, and you know, people see what you check out, they see what you carry. I’d have folders and scraps and notes, and then somebody looks at you a certain way and it just— it felt planned. Like they knew. Even if nobody else believed me.

And I try to ignore it, you know, like maybe it’s the neighbors or the TV, but I don’t even have anything on sometimes. My place is small, so you can tell when someone’s there… and nobody is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being controlled or that someone is trying to communicate with you in ways others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like somebody has a remote control in my head exactly, but I did get it in my head that people were… watching what I was doing. The other home-care agency, and some people from the library too, like they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to steal some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nAnd I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but… see, the library people know everybody, they see what you check out, and I used to go there to look at art books sometimes. Anyway, I just knew they were doing it. I could feel it, like they were putting things together behind my back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts were being placed in your mind that aren't your own, or that events or actions around you were specifically directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head, like… I can’t say it that way exactly. Like that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, they were watching what I was doing—my paintings, my client papers, all of it.\n\nI used to go to the library for art books, and you know how people there see what you’re checking out, who you talk to… it all connects. Folks told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but I still knew they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. I could feel it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you—like their actions or conversations—are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s kind of what I mean. I don’t know about thoughts being taken out like that… I mean, not exactly. What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.

I knew they were doing it, even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like things on TV, radio, or even random events—were specifically meant to send you a message or were directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was like the TV or radio talking straight to me… I don’t really watch the same shows every day, and sometimes the commercials all sound the same anyway. I used to go to the library for art books, and you know, people notice what you’re interested in. I could just tell they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions were being controlled by an outside force, or that thoughts were being put into your mind that didn’t belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think controlled, no… not like somebody had a remote on me or anything. I get thoughts, same as anybody, and sometimes I think too much, especially when I’m tired or when people keep asking me things and I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nLike with work, you know, when you’re going from house to house and everybody wants something different, it gets in your head. But I can’t say somebody else was putting thoughts in there. I don’t know what that would even feel like, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs about yourself that others might not understand, such as thinking you have special powers, a special mission, or are someone important that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. But I do hear voices. That’s really why I came in, because it’s gotten so clear, like somebody is right there in my little studio with me.

They’ll say my name, and then it’s like they’re saying my work is being judged. My paintings, I mean. And the house will be quiet — nobody there, no TV or anything — and I still hear it.

Sometimes I try to tell myself it’s just the neighbors or something, because sound carries funny, you know, especially when you’re working and you’re tired… but it sounds right there, like a person standing close by.

And then I felt like they were putting it together, like trying to steal a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying it didn’t make sense, but… I still knew. I could just tell they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions were being controlled by an outside force, or that thoughts were being put into your mind that didn’t belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think controlled, no… not like somebody had a remote on me or anything. And thoughts being put in my head, I don’t know. I get thoughts, same as anybody, and sometimes I think too much, especially when I’m tired or when people keep asking me things and I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nLike with work, you know, when you’re going from house to house and everybody wants something different, it gets in your head. But I can’t say somebody else was putting thoughts in there. I don’t know what that would even feel like, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs about yourself that others might not understand, such as thinking you have special powers, a special mission, or are someone important that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I used to get art books there too, and then I’d think, well, now they know what I’m working on.\n\nSo yes, I thought they were talking about me and trying to take an opportunity that was meant for me. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.\n\nAnd I know people said, “That doesn’t make sense, why would they do that,” but at the time I was sure. I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel completely certain about something happening to you or being planned against you, even when others say there's no evidence it's true?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what I mean. I got really certain that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching what I was doing.\n\nIt started with my paintings, but then it felt like they were noticing my client papers too, like they were putting things together and trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. And I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d try to hear that, but I still felt like… no, I know what I’m seeing.\n\nThe library thing bothered me because I used to go there for art books, and you know, people see what you check out, they see what you carry. I’d have folders and scraps and notes, and then somebody looks at you a certain way and it just— it felt planned. Like they knew. Even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being taken out like that… I mean, not exactly. What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people at the library — I got it in my head they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to figure something out and take a chance that was meant for me. And people told me, “That makes no sense,” but at the time I still knew they were doing it.\n\nThe library, you know, people see what you carry, what books you’re looking at… so it felt like they knew things. Not so much my thoughts flying out, more like they were watching and putting it together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced sensations on your body that you couldn’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. But I do hear voices. That’s really why I came in, because it’s gotten so clear, like somebody is right there in my little studio with me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s like they’re saying my work is being judged. My paintings, I mean. And the house will be quiet — nobody there, no TV or anything — and I still hear it.\n\nSometimes I try to tell myself it’s just the neighbors or something, because sound carries funny, you know, especially when you’re working and you’re tired… but it sounds right there, like a person standing close by.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people at the library — I got it in my head they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to figure something out and take a chance that was meant for me. And people told me, “That makes no sense,” but at the time I still knew they were doing it.\n\nThe library, you know, people see what you carry, what books you’re looking at… so it felt like they knew things. Not so much my thoughts flying out, more like they were watching and putting it together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced sensations on your body that you couldn’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. But I do hear voices. That’s really why I came in, because it’s gotten so clear, like somebody is right there in my little studio with me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s like they’re saying my work is being judged. My paintings, I mean. And the house will be quiet — nobody there, no TV or anything — and I still hear it.\n\nSometimes I try to tell myself it’s just the neighbors or something, because sound carries funny, you know, especially when you’re working and you’re tired… but it sounds right there, like a person standing close by.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV or something that happened on the street, were directly related to you or sending you a special message?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I got it in my head that the other home-care agency and some people from the library were watching my paintings and client papers to steal a chance that was meant for me, and even when folks told me it made no sense I still knew they were doing it.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, not so much TV, I don’t watch a whole lot besides whatever’s on when I’m cleaning up or eating. But out in places, like the library, little things started feeling like they were about me.

Like if somebody looked at my folder, or if they were near the art books after I’d been there, I’d think, okay, they know. They’re watching the paintings. And then with the home-care agency too, it felt connected, like they were looking at my client papers and putting things together to steal some chance that was supposed to be mine.

People kept saying, “That doesn’t make sense,” but at the time I couldn’t let it go. I felt sure. Even the way people moved around or looked over — it felt like it meant something about me.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… that’s kind of what I mean. I don’t know if “important” is the word, but I got it in my head that my paintings were being watched, like people could see there was something there.

And it wasn’t just the paintings, it was my client papers too, from the home-care work. That other agency, and then some people at the library — because I go there, I look at art books, I carry my folders around — it felt like they were putting things together. Like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.

People told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I know how it sounds when I say it out loud. But at the time I still knew they were doing it. I couldn’t shake it.

But I did feel like things around me were about me, yes. Like that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, they were watching what I was doing—my paintings, my client papers, all of it.\n\nI used to go to the library for art books, and you know how people there see what you’re checking out, who you talk to… it all connects. Folks told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but I still knew they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. I could feel it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you—like their actions or conversations—are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s kind of what I mean. Like I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some of those people from the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, even my client papers… like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nAnd people told me, “That doesn’t make any sense,” but they don’t know how people talk, you know? At the library everybody sees things, who comes in, what books you look at… I used to look at art books there, and then it just felt like it all connected. I knew they were doing it, even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like things on TV, radio, or even random events—were specifically meant to send you a message or were directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was like the TV or radio talking straight to me… I don’t really watch the same shows every day, and sometimes the commercials all sound the same anyway. But I did feel like things around me were aimed at me, yes.\n\nLike I said, that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, I got it in my head they were watching what I was doing—my paintings, my client papers, all that. I used to go to the library for art books, and you know, people notice what you’re interested in. And then I felt like they were putting it together, like trying to steal a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying it didn’t make sense, but… I still knew. I could just tell they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions were being controlled by an outside force, or that thoughts were being put into your mind that didn’t belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think controlled, no… not like somebody had a remote on me or anything. And thoughts being put in my head, I don’t know. I get thoughts, same as anybody, and sometimes I think too much, especially when I’m tired or when people keep asking me things and I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nLike with work, you know, when you’re going from house to house and everybody wants something different, it gets in your head. But I can’t say somebody else was putting thoughts in there. I don’t know what that would even feel like, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs about yourself that others might not understand, such as thinking you have special powers, a special mission, or are someone important that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I used to get art books there too, and then I’d think, well, now they know what I’m working on.\n\nSo yes, I thought they were talking about me and trying to take an opportunity that was meant for me. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.\n\nAnd I know people said, “That doesn’t make sense, why would they do that,” but at the time I was sure. I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel completely certain about something happening to you or being planned against you, even when others say there's no evidence it's true?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what I mean. I got really certain that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching what I was doing.\n\nIt started with my paintings, but then it felt like they were noticing my client papers too, like they were putting things together and trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. And I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d try to hear that, but I still felt like… no, I know what I’m seeing.\n\nThe library thing bothered me because I used to go there for art books, and you know, people see what you check out, they see what you carry. I’d have folders and scraps and notes, and then somebody looks at you a certain way and it just— it felt planned. Like they knew. Even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being taken out like that… I mean, not exactly. What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people at the library — I got it in my head they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to figure something out and take a chance that was meant for me. And people told me, “That makes no sense,” but at the time I still knew they were doing it.\n\nThe library, you know, people see what you carry, what books you’re looking at… so it felt like they knew things. Not so much my thoughts flying out, more like they were watching and putting it together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced sensations on your body that you couldn’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. But I do hear voices. That’s really why I came in, because it’s gotten so clear, like somebody is right there in my little studio with me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s like they’re saying my work is being judged. My paintings, I mean. And the house will be quiet — nobody there, no TV or anything — and I still hear it.\n\nSometimes I try to tell myself it’s just the neighbors or something, because sound carries funny, you know, especially when you’re working and you’re tired… but it sounds right there, like a person standing close by.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV or something that happened on the street, were directly related to you or sending you a special message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not so much TV, I don’t watch a whole lot besides whatever’s on when I’m cleaning up or eating. But out in places, like the library, little things started feeling like they were about me.\n\nLike if somebody looked at my folder, or if they were near the art books after I’d been there, I’d think, okay, they know. They’re watching the paintings. And then with the home-care agency too, it felt connected, like they were looking at my client papers and putting things together to steal some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying, “That doesn’t make sense,” but at the time I couldn’t let it go. I felt sure. Even the way people moved around or looked over — it felt like it meant something about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your artwork or your ideas were so important that they would attract special attention from people who wanted to use them for themselves?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of what I mean. Yeah… yes, I did. Like if people were talking low or moving papers around, I’d feel like it had to do with me, even if nobody said my name.

Folks told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but I still knew they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. I could feel it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you—like their actions or conversations—are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s kind of what I mean. Like I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some of those people from the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, even my client papers… like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nAnd people told me, “That doesn’t make any sense,” but they don’t know how people talk, you know? At the library everybody sees things, who comes in, what books you look at… I used to look at art books there, and then it just felt like it all connected. I knew they were doing it, even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like things on TV, radio, or even random events—were specifically meant to send you a message or were directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was like the TV or radio talking straight to me… I don’t really watch the same shows every day, and sometimes the commercials all sound the same anyway. But I did feel like things around me were aimed at me, yes.\n\nLike I said, that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, I got it in my head they were watching what I was doing—my paintings, my client papers, all that. I used to go to the library for art books, and you know, people notice what you’re interested in. And then I felt like they were putting it together, like trying to steal a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying it didn’t make sense, but… I still knew. I could just tell they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions were being controlled by an outside force, or that thoughts were being put into your mind that didn’t belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think controlled, no… not like somebody had a remote on me or anything. And thoughts being put in my head, I don’t know. I get thoughts, same as anybody, and sometimes I think too much, especially when I’m tired or when people keep asking me things and I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nLike with work, you know, when you’re going from house to house and everybody wants something different, it gets in your head. But I can’t say somebody else was putting thoughts in there. I don’t know what that would even feel like, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs about yourself that others might not understand, such as thinking you have special powers, a special mission, or are someone important that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I used to get art books there too, and then I’d think, well, now they know what I’m working on.\n\nSo yes, I thought they were talking about me and trying to take an opportunity that was meant for me. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.\n\nAnd I know people said, “That doesn’t make sense, why would they do that,” but at the time I was sure. I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel completely certain about something happening to you or being planned against you, even when others say there's no evidence it's true?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what I mean. I got really certain that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching what I was doing.\n\nIt started with my paintings, but then it felt like they were noticing my client papers too, like they were putting things together and trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. And I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d try to hear that, but I still felt like… no, I know what I’m seeing.\n\nThe library thing bothered me because I used to go there for art books, and you know, people see what you check out, they see what you carry. I’d have folders and scraps and notes, and then somebody looks at you a certain way and it just— it felt planned. Like they knew. Even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being taken out like that… I mean, not exactly. What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people at the library — I got it in my head they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to figure something out and take a chance that was meant for me. And people told me, “That makes no sense,” but at the time I still knew they were doing it.\n\nThe library, you know, people see what you carry, what books you’re looking at… so it felt like they knew things. Not so much my thoughts flying out, more like they were watching and putting it together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced sensations on your body that you couldn’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. But I do hear voices. That’s really why I came in, because it’s gotten so clear, like somebody is right there in my little studio with me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s like they’re saying my work is being judged. My paintings, I mean. And the house will be quiet — nobody there, no TV or anything — and I still hear it.\n\nSometimes I try to tell myself it’s just the neighbors or something, because sound carries funny, you know, especially when you’re working and you’re tired… but it sounds right there, like a person standing close by.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV or something that happened on the street, were directly related to you or sending you a special message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not so much TV, I don’t watch a whole lot besides whatever’s on when I’m cleaning up or eating. But out in places, like the library, little things started feeling like they were about me.\n\nLike if somebody looked at my folder, or if they were near the art books after I’d been there, I’d think, okay, they know. They’re watching the paintings. And then with the home-care agency too, it felt connected, like they were looking at my client papers and putting things together to steal some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying, “That doesn’t make sense,” but at the time I couldn’t let it go. I felt sure. Even the way people moved around or looked over — it felt like it meant something about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your artwork or your ideas were so important that they would attract special attention from people who wanted to use them for themselves?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of what I mean. I don’t know if “important” is the word, but I got it in my head that my paintings were being watched, like people could see there was something there.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just the paintings, it was my client papers too, from the home-care work. That other agency, and then some people at the library — because I go there, I look at art books, I carry my folders around — it felt like they were putting things together. Like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I know how it sounds when I say it out loud. But at the time I still knew they were doing it. I couldn’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you — like their gestures, actions, or conversations — are directly related to you, even if they don’t involve you directly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. Like if people were talking low or moving papers around, I’d feel like it had to do with me, even if nobody said my name.\n\nIt was mostly with the other home-care agency and then some people at the library. I’d be there with my folders, you know, and I’d see somebody looking near the art books or handling papers, and my mind would go straight to, they’re watching my paintings, they’re looking at my client papers. Like they were trying to steal some opportunity that was meant for me.\n\nAnd I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but… at the time, I still felt like I knew. I couldn’t just drop it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… the voices, mostly. That’s actually why I came in, because I keep hearing them like they’re right there in my little studio, clear as somebody standing next to me.

They’ll say my name, and then it’s about my work being judged, like the paintings or what I’m doing isn’t just mine anymore. And I’ll stop and look around, because the house is quiet, nobody’s there. Sometimes I think maybe it’s the pipes or somebody outside, but it’s too clear for that.

I don’t know about seeing things… it’s more the hearing. And then I get all turned around trying to explain it, because I’ll be thinking about the studio and the canvases and how I should’ve cleaned up better, and then the voice happens again. But yes, I hear it.

Anyway, I just knew they were doing it. Like that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, they were watching what I was doing—my paintings, my client papers, all of it.\n\nI used to go to the library for art books, and you know how people there see what you’re checking out, who you talk to… it all connects. My paintings, even my client papers… like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nAnd people told me, “That doesn’t make any sense,” but they don’t know how people talk, you know? At the library everybody sees things, who comes in, what books you look at… I used to look at art books there, and then it just felt like it all connected. I knew they were doing it, even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like things on TV, radio, or even random events—were specifically meant to send you a message or were directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was like the TV or radio talking straight to me… I don’t really watch the same shows every day, and sometimes the commercials all sound the same anyway. But I did feel like things around me were aimed at me, yes.\n\nLike I said, that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, I got it in my head they were watching what I was doing—my paintings, my client papers, all that. I used to go to the library for art books, and you know, people notice what you’re interested in. And then I felt like they were putting it together, like trying to steal a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying it didn’t make sense, but… I still knew. I could just tell they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions were being controlled by an outside force, or that thoughts were being put into your mind that didn’t belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think controlled, no… not like somebody had a remote on me or anything. And thoughts being put in my head, I don’t know. I get thoughts, same as anybody, and sometimes I think too much, especially when I’m tired or when people keep asking me things and I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nLike with work, you know, when you’re going from house to house and everybody wants something different, it gets in your head. But I can’t say somebody else was putting thoughts in there. I don’t know what that would even feel like, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs about yourself that others might not understand, such as thinking you have special powers, a special mission, or are someone important that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I used to get art books there too, and then I’d think, well, now they know what I’m working on.\n\nSo yes, I thought they were talking about me and trying to take an opportunity that was meant for me. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.\n\nAnd I know people said, “That doesn’t make sense, why would they do that,” but at the time I was sure. I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel completely certain about something happening to you or being planned against you, even when others say there's no evidence it's true?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what I mean. I got really certain that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching what I was doing.\n\nIt started with my paintings, but then it felt like they were noticing my client papers too, like they were putting things together and trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. And I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d try to hear that, but I still felt like… no, I know what I’m seeing.\n\nThe library thing bothered me because I used to go there for art books, and you know, people see what you check out, they see what you carry. I’d have folders and scraps and notes, and then somebody looks at you a certain way and it just— it felt planned. Like they knew. Even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being taken out like that… I mean, not exactly. What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people at the library — I got it in my head they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to figure something out and take a chance that was meant for me. And people told me, “That makes no sense,” but at the time I still knew they were doing it.\n\nThe library, you know, people see what you carry, what books you’re looking at… so it felt like they knew things. Not so much my thoughts flying out, more like they were watching and putting it together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced sensations on your body that you couldn’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. And thoughts being put in my head, I don’t know. I get thoughts, same as anybody, and sometimes I think too much, especially when I’m tired or when people keep asking me things and I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nLike with work, you know, when you’re going from house to house and everybody wants something different, it gets in your head. But I can’t say somebody else was putting thoughts in there. I don’t know what that would even feel like, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs about yourself that others might not understand, such as thinking you have special powers, a special mission, or are someone important that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I used to get art books there too, and then I’d think, well, now they know what I’m working on.\n\nSo yes, I thought they were talking about me and trying to take an opportunity that was meant for me. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.\n\nAnd I know people said, “That doesn’t make sense, why would they do that,” but at the time I was sure. I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel completely certain about something happening to you or being planned against you, even when others say there's no evidence it's true?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what I mean. I got really certain that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching what I was doing.\n\nIt started with my paintings, but then it felt like they were noticing my client papers too, like they were putting things together and trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. And I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d try to hear that, but I still felt like… no, I know what I’m seeing.\n\nThe library thing bothered me because I used to go there for art books, and you know, people see what you check out, they see what you carry. I’d have folders and scraps and notes, and then somebody looks at you a certain way and it just— it felt planned. Like they knew. Even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being taken out like that… I mean, not exactly. What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people at the library — I got it in my head they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to figure something out and take a chance that was meant for me. And people told me, “That makes no sense,” but at the time I still knew they were doing it.\n\nThe library, you know, people see what you carry, what books you’re looking at… so it felt like they knew things. Not so much my thoughts flying out, more like they were watching and putting it together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced sensations on your body that you couldn’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. But I do hear voices. That’s really why I came in, because it’s gotten so clear, like somebody is right there in my little studio with me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s like they’re saying my work is being judged. My paintings, I mean. And the house will be quiet — nobody there, no TV or anything — and I still hear it.\n\nSometimes I try to tell myself it’s just the neighbors or something, because sound carries funny, you know, especially when you’re working and you’re tired… but it sounds right there, like a person standing close by.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV or something that happened on the street, were directly related to you or sending you a special message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not so much TV, I don’t watch a whole lot besides whatever’s on when I’m cleaning up or eating. But out in places, like the library, little things started feeling like they were about me.\n\nLike if somebody looked at my folder, or if they were near the art books after I’d been there, I’d think, okay, they know. They’re watching the paintings. And then with the home-care agency too, it felt connected, like they were looking at my client papers and putting things together to steal some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying, “That doesn’t make sense,” but at the time I couldn’t let it go. I felt sure. Even the way people moved around or looked over — it felt like it meant something about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your artwork or your ideas were so important that they would attract special attention from people who wanted to use them for themselves?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of what I mean. I don’t know if “important” is the word, but I got it in my head that my paintings were being watched, like people could see there was something there.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just the paintings, it was my client papers too, from the home-care work. That other agency, and then some people at the library — because I go there, I look at art books, I carry my folders around — it felt like they were putting things together. Like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I know how it sounds when I say it out loud. But at the time I still knew they were doing it. I couldn’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you — like their gestures, actions, or conversations — are directly related to you, even if they don’t involve you directly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. Like if people were talking low or moving papers around, I’d feel like it had to do with me, even if nobody said my name.\n\nIt was mostly with the other home-care agency and then some people at the library. I’d be there with my folders, you know, and I’d see somebody looking near the art books or handling papers, and my mind would go straight to, they’re watching my paintings, they’re looking at my client papers. Like they were trying to steal some opportunity that was meant for me.\n\nAnd I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but… at the time, I still felt like I knew. I couldn’t just drop it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other symptoms such as seeing things that aren’t there, feeling sensations on your body with no explanation, or having trouble organizing your thoughts or speech?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the voices, mostly. That’s actually why I came in, because I keep hearing them like they’re right there in my little studio, clear as somebody standing next to me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s about my work being judged, like the paintings or what I’m doing isn’t just mine anymore. And I’ll stop and look around, because the house is quiet, nobody’s there. Sometimes I think maybe it’s the pipes or somebody outside, but it’s too clear for that.\n\nI don’t know about seeing things… it’s more the hearing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know about a light flickering exactly… I mean, sometimes I’d notice things and my mind would go somewhere with it, but the big thing was more people and papers.

Like that other home-care agency, and then some people at the library — because I’d be there with my art books and my folders, and I had my client papers too, which I probably shouldn’t have been carrying around so much, but I was always trying to keep things straight. And I started feeling like they were watching my paintings and looking at my papers, like they knew there was some chance coming for me and they were trying to take it.

People would say, “Why would they do that?” or “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d hear them, but I still knew it. It felt like it was meant for me and they were moving around it, trying to get it first.
