I could feel it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you—like their actions or conversations—are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s kind of what I mean. Like I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some of those people from the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, even my client papers… like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nAnd people told me, “That doesn’t make any sense,” but they don’t know how people talk, you know? At the library everybody sees things, who comes in, what books you look at… I used to look at art books there, and then it just felt like it all connected. I knew they were doing it, even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like things on TV, radio, or even random events—were specifically meant to send you a message or were directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was like the TV or radio talking straight to me… I don’t really watch the same shows every day, and sometimes the commercials all sound the same anyway. But I did feel like things around me were aimed at me, yes.\n\nLike I said, that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, I got it in my head they were watching what I was doing—my paintings, my client papers, all that. I used to go to the library for art books, and you know, people notice what you’re interested in. And then I felt like they were putting it together, like trying to steal a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying it didn’t make sense, but… I still knew. I could just tell they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions were being controlled by an outside force, or that thoughts were being put into your mind that didn’t belong to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think controlled, no… not like somebody had a remote on me or anything. And thoughts being put in my head, I don’t know. I get thoughts, same as anybody, and sometimes I think too much, especially when I’m tired or when people keep asking me things and I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nLike with work, you know, when you’re going from house to house and everybody wants something different, it gets in your head. But I can’t say somebody else was putting thoughts in there. I don’t know what that would even feel like, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs about yourself that others might not understand, such as thinking you have special powers, a special mission, or are someone important that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it special powers, not like that. But I did feel like I had something important that people weren’t seeing yet.\n\nWith my artwork, I really believed it was going to get discovered any day, like somebody was going to come across it and say, “This is the one.” I’d be working on pieces and thinking, this is bigger than just me doing paintings at home, you know? And then with the care work… I had this idea that I was supposed to put it together, like a whole care-and-art program. Not just activities, but something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nBecause I know what it’s like in people’s homes, and I know how art can calm people down or make them remember things, and—well, I used to keep scraps and notes and all kinds of things, and my kitchen table would get covered, then I’d have to move it because I needed to eat. But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. I used to get art books there too, and then I’d think, well, now they know what I’m working on.\n\nSo yes, I thought they were talking about me and trying to take an opportunity that was meant for me. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.\n\nAnd I know people said, “That doesn’t make sense, why would they do that,” but at the time I was sure. I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel completely certain about something happening to you or being planned against you, even when others say there's no evidence it's true?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what I mean. I got really certain that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching what I was doing.\n\nIt started with my paintings, but then it felt like they were noticing my client papers too, like they were putting things together and trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. And I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d try to hear that, but I still felt like… no, I know what I’m seeing.\n\nThe library thing bothered me because I used to go there for art books, and you know, people see what you check out, they see what you carry. I’d have folders and scraps and notes, and then somebody looks at you a certain way and it just— it felt planned. Like they knew. Even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being taken out like that… I mean, not exactly. What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people at the library — I got it in my head they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to figure something out and take a chance that was meant for me. And people told me, “That makes no sense,” but at the time I still knew they were doing it.\n\nThe library, you know, people see what you carry, what books you’re looking at… so it felt like they knew things. Not so much my thoughts flying out, more like they were watching and putting it together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced sensations on your body that you couldn’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. But I do hear voices. That’s really why I came in, because it’s gotten so clear, like somebody is right there in my little studio with me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s like they’re saying my work is being judged. My paintings, I mean. And the house will be quiet — nobody there, no TV or anything — and I still hear it.\n\nSometimes I try to tell myself it’s just the neighbors or something, because sound carries funny, you know, especially when you’re working and you’re tired… but it sounds right there, like a person standing close by.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV or something that happened on the street, were directly related to you or sending you a special message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not so much TV, I don’t watch a whole lot besides whatever’s on when I’m cleaning up or eating. But out in places, like the library, little things started feeling like they were about me.\n\nLike if somebody looked at my folder, or if they were near the art books after I’d been there, I’d think, okay, they know. They’re watching the paintings. And then with the home-care agency too, it felt connected, like they were looking at my client papers and putting things together to steal some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying, “That doesn’t make sense,” but at the time I couldn’t let it go. I felt sure. Even the way people moved around or looked over — it felt like it meant something about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your artwork or your ideas were so important that they would attract special attention from people who wanted to use them for themselves?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of what I mean. I don’t know if “important” is the word, but I got it in my head that my paintings were being watched, like people could see there was something there.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just the paintings, it was my client papers too, from the home-care work. That other agency, and then some people at the library — because I go there, I look at art books, I carry my folders around — it felt like they were putting things together. Like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I know how it sounds when I say it out loud. But at the time I still knew they were doing it. I couldn’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you — like their gestures, actions, or conversations — are directly related to you, even if they don’t involve you directly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. Like if people were talking low or moving papers around, I’d feel like it had to do with me, even if nobody said my name.\n\nIt was mostly with the other home-care agency and then some people at the library. I’d be there with my folders, you know, and I’d see somebody looking near the art books or handling papers, and my mind would go straight to, they’re watching my paintings, they’re looking at my client papers. Like they were trying to steal some opportunity that was meant for me.\n\nAnd I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but… at the time, I still felt like I knew. I couldn’t just drop it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other symptoms such as seeing things that aren’t there, feeling sensations on your body with no explanation, or having trouble organizing your thoughts or speech?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the voices, mostly. That’s actually why I came in, because I keep hearing them like they’re right there in my little studio, clear as somebody standing next to me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s about my work being judged, like the paintings or what I’m doing isn’t just mine anymore. And I’ll stop and look around, because the house is quiet, nobody’s there. Sometimes I think maybe it’s the pipes or somebody outside, but it’s too clear for that.\n\nI don’t know about seeing things… it’s more the hearing. And then I get all turned around trying to explain it, because I’ll be thinking about the studio and the canvases and how I should’ve cleaned up better, and then the voice happens again. But yes, I hear it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things like objects, events, or even inanimate things — like how a light flickers or how a car drives by — were sending you a message or had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a light flickering exactly… I mean, sometimes I’d notice things and my mind would go somewhere with it, but the big thing was more people and papers.\n\nLike that other home-care agency, and then some people at the library — because I’d be there with my art books and my folders, and I had my client papers too, which I probably shouldn’t have been carrying around so much, but I was always trying to keep things straight. And I started feeling like they were watching my paintings and looking at my papers, like they knew there was some chance coming for me and they were trying to take it.\n\nPeople would say, “Why would they do that?” or “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d hear them, but I still knew it. It felt like it was meant for me and they were moving around it, trying to get it first.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I don’t know about thoughts being put in or taken out like that… not exactly. It was more like I got something in my head and I couldn’t shake it.

It felt like it was meant for me and they were moving around it, trying to get it first.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, taken out of your mind, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without intending to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in or taken out like that… not exactly. It was more like I got something in my head and I couldn’t shake it.\n\nLike with that other home-care agency, and then the people at the library — I’d be trying to keep my folders together, my paintings, my client papers, and I’d start thinking they were watching all of it. Like they knew there was a chance meant for me, something with my work maybe, and they were trying to get to it first.\n\nAnd people would tell me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but it didn’t matter. I still felt like I knew they were doing it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that your artwork or your ideas were so special or important that they deserved recognition or attention from others that they weren’t getting?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Inflated_Self_Esteem\nmanifestation: \"I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I did feel that way. I really thought my paintings were going to be discovered any day, like somebody was going to finally see what I was doing and understand it was important.

And it wasn’t just the art by itself. I had this whole idea in my head about combining care work with art, because I’ve done personal care and I know how people get overlooked, you know? I thought I was supposed to build this whole care-and-art program, and doctors and agencies would come learn from me. Like I had figured out something they hadn’t.

I know that might sound big, but at the time it felt true. Like I was waiting for everyone else to catch up.

But yes, I really thought it was going to happen, like I was meant to do it and people just didn’t understand yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that other people were talking about you, following you, or trying to harm you in ways beyond what you’ve already described—like in other parts of your life or in different situations?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was that same kind of thing, with the agency and the library people. I can’t say somebody was following me everywhere, like in a movie or something, but I felt watched. Like they were noticing my paintings, and even my client papers, and putting things together behind my back.\n\nAnd see, that’s what people don’t understand, because when you’re a care aide you carry things around, notes and schedules and folders, and sometimes you stop at the library or the store, and people look. They act like they’re not looking, but they look. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, I mean. People told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that something was happening or being done to you that others said wasn’t true, even though you were completely sure it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happened. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing. My paintings, and even my client papers, like they were looking at them and putting pieces together.\n\nAnd I know people said, “That doesn’t make sense, why would they do that,” but at the time I was sure. I mean, when you’re carrying folders around and you stop at the library, and people know you come in for art books, and then they look at you a certain way… it felt like they were trying to take a chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept telling me I was wrong, but I still felt like I knew.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel completely certain about something happening to you or being planned against you, even when others say there's no evidence it's true?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what I mean. I got really certain that the other home-care agency, and some people from the library, were watching what I was doing.\n\nIt started with my paintings, but then it felt like they were noticing my client papers too, like they were putting things together and trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. And I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d try to hear that, but I still felt like… no, I know what I’m seeing.\n\nThe library thing bothered me because I used to go there for art books, and you know, people see what you check out, they see what you carry. I’d have folders and scraps and notes, and then somebody looks at you a certain way and it just— it felt planned. Like they knew. Even if nobody else believed me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being taken out like that… I mean, not exactly. What I keep coming back to is more that people were watching what I was doing.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people at the library — I got it in my head they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers, trying to figure something out and take a chance that was meant for me. And people told me, “That makes no sense,” but at the time I still knew they were doing it.\n\nThe library, you know, people see what you carry, what books you’re looking at… so it felt like they knew things. Not so much my thoughts flying out, more like they were watching and putting it together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced sensations on your body that you couldn’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about seeing things, not like… shapes or people standing there. But I do hear voices. That’s really why I came in, because it’s gotten so clear, like somebody is right there in my little studio with me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s like they’re saying my work is being judged. My paintings, I mean. And the house will be quiet — nobody there, no TV or anything — and I still hear it.\n\nSometimes I try to tell myself it’s just the neighbors or something, because sound carries funny, you know, especially when you’re working and you’re tired… but it sounds right there, like a person standing close by.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, like something you saw on TV or something that happened on the street, were directly related to you or sending you a special message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not so much TV, I don’t watch a whole lot besides whatever’s on when I’m cleaning up or eating. But out in places, like the library, little things started feeling like they were about me.\n\nLike if somebody looked at my folder, or if they were near the art books after I’d been there, I’d think, okay, they know. They’re watching the paintings. And then with the home-care agency too, it felt connected, like they were looking at my client papers and putting things together to steal some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople kept saying, “That doesn’t make sense,” but at the time I couldn’t let it go. I felt sure. Even the way people moved around or looked over — it felt like it meant something about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your artwork or your ideas were so important that they would attract special attention from people who wanted to use them for themselves?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of what I mean. I don’t know if “important” is the word, but I got it in my head that my paintings were being watched, like people could see there was something there.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just the paintings, it was my client papers too, from the home-care work. That other agency, and then some people at the library — because I go there, I look at art books, I carry my folders around — it felt like they were putting things together. Like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.\n\nPeople told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” and I know how it sounds when I say it out loud. But at the time I still knew they were doing it. I couldn’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you — like their gestures, actions, or conversations — are directly related to you, even if they don’t involve you directly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. Like if people were talking low or moving papers around, I’d feel like it had to do with me, even if nobody said my name.\n\nIt was mostly with the other home-care agency and then some people at the library. I’d be there with my folders, you know, and I’d see somebody looking near the art books or handling papers, and my mind would go straight to, they’re watching my paintings, they’re looking at my client papers. Like they were trying to steal some opportunity that was meant for me.\n\nAnd I know people told me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but… at the time, I still felt like I knew. I couldn’t just drop it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other symptoms such as seeing things that aren’t there, feeling sensations on your body with no explanation, or having trouble organizing your thoughts or speech?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the voices, mostly. That’s actually why I came in, because I keep hearing them like they’re right there in my little studio, clear as somebody standing next to me.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then it’s about my work being judged, like the paintings or what I’m doing isn’t just mine anymore. And I’ll stop and look around, because the house is quiet, nobody’s there. Sometimes I think maybe it’s the pipes or somebody outside, but it’s too clear for that.\n\nI don’t know about seeing things… it’s more the hearing. And then I get all turned around trying to explain it, because I’ll be thinking about the studio and the canvases and how I should’ve cleaned up better, and then the voice happens again. But yes, I hear it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things like objects, events, or even inanimate things — like how a light flickers or how a car drives by — were sending you a message or had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a light flickering exactly… I mean, sometimes I’d notice things and my mind would go somewhere with it, but the big thing was more people and papers.\n\nLike that other home-care agency, and then some people at the library — because I’d be there with my art books and my folders, and I had my client papers too, which I probably shouldn’t have been carrying around so much, but I was always trying to keep things straight. And I started feeling like they were watching my paintings and looking at my papers, like they knew there was some chance coming for me and they were trying to take it.\n\nPeople would say, “Why would they do that?” or “That doesn’t make sense,” and I’d hear them, but I still knew it. It felt like it was meant for me and they were moving around it, trying to get it first.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, taken out of your mind, or that you were broadcasting your thoughts to others without intending to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in or taken out like that… not exactly. It was more like I got something in my head and I couldn’t shake it.\n\nLike with that other home-care agency, and then the people at the library — I’d be trying to keep my folders together, my paintings, my client papers, and I’d start thinking they were watching all of it. Like they knew there was a chance meant for me, something with my work maybe, and they were trying to get to it first.\n\nAnd people would tell me, “That doesn’t make sense,” but it didn’t matter. I still felt like I knew they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your artwork or your ideas were so special or important that they deserved recognition or attention from others that they weren’t getting?\n\nPatient: Yes… I did feel that way. I really thought my paintings were going to be discovered any day, like somebody was going to finally see what I was doing and understand it was important.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just the art by itself. I had this whole idea in my head about combining care work with art, because I’ve done personal care and I know how people get overlooked, you know? I thought I was supposed to build this whole care-and-art program, and doctors and agencies would come learn from me. Like I had figured out something they hadn’t.\n\nI know that might sound big, but at the time it felt true. Like I was waiting for everyone else to catch up."
